| E38 | `CollateralWarning` | warning | there were sessions in which short collateral was insufficient and new short sales and long buys were blocked (cash account: no forced-liquidation model; margin account in 5.6.7a [R6-07]) [V31-LS-04]; sessions in which mandatory obligations made cash negative (`shortfall`); sleeve ruin `ruined` [R3-09] | "collateral short by {amt} {ccy} on {d} sessions (max {x})" · "mandatory debits left cash negative on {d} sessions (min {x})" · "sleeve {m} ruined on {date}: new orders stopped" / "lower [shorting] max_gross or collateral_ratio; forced buy-ins are not modeled" |
| E39 | `UnknownFilterNotice` | notice | during a run, the unknown share of a universe Filter > 50% at a decision and market (5.4.1) [R3-13] | "{p}% of listed names are unknown for universe filter {leaf} on {n} decisions (input NaN: {column})" / "make the fallback explicit: ({leaf}).fillna(True) or .fillna(False)" |
| E40 | `ValidationNotice` | notice | a CPCV with nothing to select reduced to 1 path, the fit count exceeds `[validate] max_fits`, a fold's training samples fewer than `min_samples` (default 100) (6.3a) [R4-20, R4-22] | "cpcv has nothing to fit or select; all paths are identical, showing 1 path" · "{n} model fits planned (> max_fits {m})" / "add grid= or an ml.factor, or use method='walk_forward'" |
| E41 | `BlockOutputError` | fail | a user block's output is outside its contract: a multiplier outside `[0, 1]` or not finite, a negative or infinite sizer output, a selected id outside the universe, account access by a `reads_account=False` rule, a state value that is not a JSON scalar (3.8, 5.5.2) [R4-07] | "{block} returned mult {x} for {id} on {d}; risk rules may only scale toward zero (0 ≤ m ≤ 1)" / "clip in your rule, or declare @nx.risk_rule(reads_account=True)" |
| E42 | `AccountNotEvaluatedNotice` | notice | `screen` meets an account rule without an account (m = 1 is used) [R4-08] | "{rule}: not evaluated (no account)" / "use --ledger BOOK or --as-backtest" |
| E43 | `SolverFallbackNotice` | notice | ERC fallback, `vol_target` not computable (5.5.1a) [R4-03] | "erc did not converge on {n} of {m} decisions; inverse_vol used (reasons a/b/c: …)" / "raise lookback, add shrink=0.1, or use size.inverse_vol" |
| E44 | `MLDeterminismWarning` | warning | `[ml] threads ≠ 1`, known non-deterministic models or GPU (5.3.11) [R4-23] | "fits used {k} threads; rerun compares predictions with tolerance 1e-9" / "[ml] threads = 1 for bit-identical reruns" |
| E45 | `SkippedWorkNotice` | notice | a skip reason without a fixed E code (6.6 no silent failure) [R4-17] | "skipped {n} {what} for reason {r}" / reason-code documentation |
| E46 | `EngineBugError` | fail (exit code 2) | invariant violation, engine assert, unexpected exception in engine code [R4-15] | "this is a NexusBT bug, not your code or data" / "please report: nexusbt bugreport {run_id}" |
| E47 | `DatasetShapeError` | fail | required columns per shape missing, wrong types or duplicate keys in `from_parquet` (the 3.2 list: events = `ts_event` + `known_on` + at least one of the instrument, issuer and series columns; series = `period_end` + `known_on` + `value`), an `Events`/`Macro` dataset that differs from the TDS 5.21 format [R4F-EV-04, R4G-04] | "events need a ts_event column, a known_on column and one of instrument_id/issuer_id/series_id; '{c}' is missing in {path}" / "nx.dataset.from_parquet(path, shape='events', ts_event='announced_at', known_on='published_at', rename={'ticker_id': 'instrument_id'}, name='my_earnings')" (a line callable exactly with the 3.2 signature; `tests/errors` actually runs this line) |
| E48 | `PretrainedLeakError` | fail | trying to use predictions for decisions before a pretrained model's `training_cutoff` (+ embargo) (5.3.11) [R5-03] | layer: your code / "model {name} was trained on data up to {cutoff}; decision {d} is before it" / "start after {cutoff+embargo}, or nx.ml.pretrained(..., allow_overlap=True) to mark instead of fail" |
| E49 | `PretrainedOverlapWarning` | warning | overlapping decisions marked with `allow_overlap=True` and the run proceeds (5.3.11) [R5-03] | layer: your code / "{n} decisions use a pretrained model that saw their period" / "read the marked rows before trusting the result" |
| E35 | `BookConflictError` | fail | the book lock is held, the book definition (`definition_sha256`) differs from the current strategy and settings, an order batch of another book or state version [RV-C13] | "book {b} is locked by pid {p}" / "book {b} was created for graph {g1}; current strategy is {g2}; create a new book" |

- An exception inside a user function raises the original exception as it is (not wrapped in an engine error), and engine frames are hidden so that the first line of the traceback is the user file:line (`__tracebackhide__`; `raise ... from None` is not used) [R-42].

## 7. Performance budgets [P1~P10, 11.8]

**Machine**: the user's compute machine = Apple M4, 16 GB memory, local SSD, macOS, with the collector running alongside as usual, at least 20 GB of free disk (addresses outside the repository, 2.7). **Data**: one fixed snapshot (the first real E1 export; before that, the synthetic `full` preset `synth-full-0001`). The snapshot id is written in the results.

| # | Task | Gate (release) | Target | Measurement definition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | `import nexusbt as nx` + opening and validating `tds.json` (`verify="manifest"`, profile judgement included) | p95 ≤ 1.5 s | 1.0 s | a new process each time, OS cache warm |
| P2 | `nx.screen(strategy)` 252-session window, KR+US | p95 ≤ 2 s | 1 s | warm process, first of 11 runs in the same process discarded |
| P3 | `nx.evaluate(Returns(232).shift(21))` monthly 30 years KR+US | p95 ≤ 5 s (no engine cache) / ≤ 1 s (cache) | 3 s / 0.5 s | new process, cache state stated |
| P4 | per-session IC factor (`freq="daily"`) 30 years US | p95 ≤ 30 s | 20 s | |
| P5 | `nx.backtest` monthly top-N (n=50/sleeve) with costs 30 years KR+US, accounting, 5 scenarios and saving included | p95 ≤ 30 s | **10 s** | new process, no engine cache |
| P6 | `nx.sweep` 27 candidates, common terms shared | p95 ≤ 120 s | 60 s | |
| P7 | peak summed RSS of the engine process tree (main + workers) during P5 | ≤ 4 GB | 3 GB | psutil samples every 50 ms, summed over the whole tree. L0 memory-mapped pages count in RSS, so the peak summed **USS (anonymous memory)** is also reported on a separate line, and the gate is also judged as USS ≤ 4 GB (both reported). If a 1-year chunk exceeds the budget, it is split smaller (5.3.8) [SC-15, RV-I05] |
| P8 | static checks (6.5) | ≤ 1 s, before heavy computation | | from the call to the start of computation |
| P9 | truncation probe | no gate, time reported | | [C8] |
| P10 | zipline comparison tolerance | table 8.1 | | correctness gate |
| P11 | **High-dimensional** [R4-18]: 300 factors (made from about 40 base columns through windows, ranks and composites) × 30 years × KR+US, monthly decisions — (a) `nx.evaluate({300 factors})`, (b) `backtest` of a 300-factor `ops.composite` score (top-N, costs and accounting included), (c) (b) with `method="walk_forward"` + a 3-point `grid` | **target only** (the gate is set by an ADR after prototype T6.26 measures it) | (a) ≤ 300 s, (b) ≤ 60 s, (c) ≤ 180 s; engine tree USS ≤ 6 GB | new process, both without and with the engine cache; bytes of columns read, decision rows computed, node count and shared node count reported alongside |

**Early measurement** [RV-I05]: the bench rig (T1.24) comes in at **M1**, and at each milestone measures the stages that exist by then: M1 = opening, block reads, adjustment, window kernels (`SMA(252)` over all daily rows, definition A; the basis for deciding whether to switch to ADR-0019 B), cross-sections; end of M3a = the state machine (one market, `full_ci` at 1/50 of synthetic `full`, and `full` on the compute machine); end of M4 = two sleeves and scenarios. M6 (T6.11) is the gate judgement. P7 memory is measured as the sum over the **whole process tree** (main + workers).

**Measurement rules** [11.8, 3.10]: `bench/run.py --workload P5 --reps 10 --snapshot <id>`. 10 or more runs, p50/p95/maximum reported, the first-process JIT delay (Numba cache emptied) on a separate line, the engine cache state stated, saving time included, no extrapolation from return arithmetic. The result JSON `bench/results/<YYYYMMDD>-<git sha>-m4.json` (workload, reps, p50, p95, max, rss_max, uss_max, snapshot_id, cache_state (hit/miss for each of L0/L1), jit_first_s, versions, `fixture_sha256`, `stage_seconds{}`, `output_value_sha256{}` (canonical output value hashes — the evidence of a result that is fast yet the same) [R4F-18] — summary figures only, no paths or instrument values, 2.7) is committed at the end of a milestone. Per-stage budget (against the P5 30-second gate [est.]): opening the snapshot 1.5, block reads 0.5 on an L0 hit and 6 on a miss, Pipeline decision rows only 3, targets 0.5, state machine (30 years × 2 sleeves) 8, 4 scenarios (gross = cost0x once) 7, saving and hashing 2 — replaced by a real table from T1.24 (M1) and measured again at the end of M3a and M4 [blueprint 12.3, RV-I05]. The state machine is column arrays (5.6.1), and T6.6 (Numba session step) is done only when measurement shows the P5 gate cannot be held [RV-I10]. If a gate cannot be held, the bottleneck is found by profiling before the table is changed and the reason is written in an ADR (a Rust implementation only after the bottleneck is confirmed, swapped in through the 2.4 seam and tested for bit identity — ADR-0016 [R3-26]) [Q14, 11.9 Q-E4].

**Workload P2b** (not a gate, reported) [V31-12]: `screen(strategy)` with L0 and L1 caches warmed by yesterday's snapshot + today's snapshot (only the last year partition changed) — the real situation of a screener used every day. Target ≤ 2 s (the same as P2), reported together with the number of changed partitions and the hit rate (the v3.1 key included `markets`, which holds the watermark, so the hit rate was 0; the v3.2 L0 key has only stable fields [R3-04]). For the sleeve and scenario column batches (5.3.8), the two values with them on and off are written in the T6.5 and T6.6 measurements [V31-11].

**Design means for high-dimensional workloads** [R4-18] (user difficulty 2: strategies with hundreds of factors were hard to test on zipline). To hold P11, existing rules are bundled and two are added:
1. **Sharing the same terms**: nodes with the same structural hash are computed once (5.3.2) — the return, window-mean and rank inputs that 300 factors use in common.
2. **Computing only on decision dates**: a node whose consumers read only decision rows is computed only on decision rows and reads only the window input rows of those (the planner propagates the row mask backwards, 5.3.2). Monthly 30 years = 360 decision rows (instead of about 7,500 daily rows).
3. **Column pruning**: only the columns the graph uses are read from Parquet (by column), and unneeded years are skipped by year partitions and row-group statistics.
4. **Year and market chunk caches**: L0 and L1 (5.3.9); the same L1 values are reused by `offsets`, `scenarios` and validation paths.
5. **Parallelism by market and factor**: workers compute independently per (market, year chunk, node chunk), and the results are gathered in sorted-index order so they are bit-identical (INV-03, INV-04). The worker count and the memory budget are in `[compute]` (5.3.8).
6. **Memory ceiling**: processing is done in year chunks with immediate reduction — `evaluate` reduces to IC and quantile statistics per decision and does not hold the whole factor value matrix (300 × 360 × about 12,000 × 8 B ≈ 10 GB) in memory, and a composite score holds only the 300 × n matrix of one decision (about 30 MB). [new]
7. **Rust seam**: window and cross-section kernels and reductions sit behind Arrow input and output (2.4), so when measurement shows Numba cannot hold the target, they are swapped (ADR-0016, bit-identity test). [the seam is v3.2; the P11 measurement is the new basis]
The prototype measurement is T6.26 (P11 on synthetic `full`), and with its result the gate is written into an ADR (0038 updated) and this table is changed.

**Prototype results (2026-10-28, summary `docs/design/prototype-results.md`)** [R4H-05]: P5 on a Mac mini M4 cold p50 5.8 s / p95 6.8 s, warm 1.6 / 3.7 s, USS 2.1 GiB (laptop M1 cold p95 19.6 s); P11 (a) 50 s, (b) 34 s, (c) 30 s (simplified version), USS ≤ 1.35 GiB; with daily ffill, financials alone would have been 194.6 GB (computed); computing only decision rows = full computation (2,190,000 values bit-identical); output hashes identical across two machines and cold/warm; hand calculations 32/32. Design means added [R4H-06]: **8. Skipping by row-group statistics** — the min/max statistics of Parquet row groups are used so that chunks of other markets and years are not read (P11 (a) 122 s → 45 s). Measurement rule added [R4H-07]: macOS cannot read the USS of other processes, so the summed tree USS of P7 is summed from **the measuring rig inside each worker process** reporting its own USS. The synthetic generator (4.8) adds the shapes missing in the comparison with real data [R4H-08]: `obs_status` no_trade and missing, `symbol_change` and `announced` events, and the `full` scale matched to real data (about 27,000 instruments, about 60 million daily bar rows).

**P11 fixed fixture** [R4F-18]: so that the comparison target does not move, the workload is pinned in a file — `bench/fixtures/p11.json`: a DAG of 300 factors (generator seed recording 40 base columns, the window length distribution {5, 20, 63, 126, 252}, the rank and composite ratios, and the node sharing rate), composite weights, period 1995–2024, label `fwd_return` 1 month, a 3-point grid (`n ∈ {20, 50, 100}`), walk-forward `train="10y", test="1y", step="1y"`, scenarios off. `fixture_sha256` = the sha256 of that file.
**P11 stage budget** (target, revised after measurement) [R4F-18]: (a) 300 s = opening and column reads 30 + shared-term computation (decision rows only) 180 + IC and quantile reduction per decision 60 + saving 30; (b) 60 s = Pipeline (not an L1 hit) 35 + composite score and targets 5 + state machine 15 + saving 5; (c) 180 s = reuse of (b)'s Pipeline 0 + 3-point grid linear runs 3 × 20 + fold IS slicing 5 + stitched OOS state machine 20 + the linear run run alongside 15 + saving and hashing 10 + slack 70. If a stage exceeds 2 times its budget: profile → cause ADR → only then the Rust seam (2.4).
**Correctness evidence**: the bench JSON includes `fixture_sha256`, per-stage times, USS and the **canonical output value hashes** (`outputs{file: value_sha256}`; the same hash as 5.9.2). Accepted only if the output hashes of a run without cache (cold) and with cache (warm) are identical (fast yet the same result) [R4F-18].

**CI performance smoke test** (GitHub runner, synthetic `small`): P2-small ≤ 3 s, P5-small ≤ 20 s. It is for catching a 5x regression and is not a gate.

---

## 8. Tests (comparison, hand calculation, properties and metamorphic relations, TDS conformance)

### 8.1 The zipline comparison suite (`tests/parity/`) [R-50, R-55, P10, D7, 11.8]

- **Environment**: `tests/parity/pyproject.toml` + its own `uv.lock`. Python 3.12, `zipline-reloaded==3.2.1`, `pandas==2.3.3`, the rest as the lock sets it. It is not mixed with the engine environment. Run: `uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite L1 --fixture small`. Results are written as Parquet, and the pytest of the engine-side `tests/parity_compare/` reads and compares them (the engine side does not import zipline) [R-64].
- **Scope** [SC-12, A-57]: the zipline comparison is a test that shows "the same value when the meaning is the same". It compares price terms, adjustment, masks and classification (L1) and backtests of the preset `zipline` (L2). **Our daily-bar OHLC adverse-side rules (5.6.4) differ in meaning from zipline, so they are not compared and are tested only with hand-calculated golden cases (G-02~G-09, G-31~G-33).** The order types themselves (limit, stop, stop-limit, day/GTC) are compared in L2-d **with a preset that imitates zipline's trigger behaviour** (the "comparison test with zipline" of user decision 9.0 is kept within this scope). The evidence file carries the sentence "parity does not imply realism" [blueprint 12.6].
- **Data**: CI = synthetic `small` (the version with prices rounded to 3 decimals). Manual on the compute machine = a subset of a real snapshot chosen by principle (not put in the repository; evidence is summary figures only, 2.7): 50 instruments × 5 years per market, filled for each category (split ≥1, reverse split ≥1, cash dividend ≥1, delisted during the period, halt ≥1, no events) from the front of the `sha256(instrument_id)` order — no selection fitted to instruments [design 4.8, no-ticker-fitted-rules].
- **The only conversion point**: `parity.make_bundle(snapshot_path, market) -> bundle_name` (TDS package → zipline bundle; it uses the same mapping table as `tds export zipline-bundle` of TDS 9.8, and calls that export once it exists). zipline sid = the sort order of that market's `instrument_id` (so ordinal ties come out the same). Instruments whose prices go beyond the price ×1000 uint32 range (about 4,290,000) are not dropped but held with a **per-instrument price scale** (×1/10ⁿ, undone by a test-side `InMemoryDailyBarReader` subclass), and the list of scaled instruments is written in the evidence (reported as a limit of the comparison scope) [RV-Q08]. splits ratio = `1/ρ`; dividends are given as (ex_date, record_date, declared_date, pay_date, amount) and zipline computes `1 − D/previous close`.
- **Time alignment**: zipline `EquityPricingLoader` shifts by one day → the output for zipline date D = NexusBT market-axis row `prev_session(D)`. Each market is run separately [11.8].
- The `InMemoryDailyBarReader` bug is worked around with a test-side subclass. zipline's known bugs are not taken as the answer: differences are written in `tests/parity/KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md` [R-55 fix]. When they differ, the hand calculation (T-25) decides (the same rule as 3.4) [V31-24]. Differences v3.1 knows: missing values under Filter `~` (we: unknown, zipline: true) [V31-21] — L1-d compares only cells without missing values, and missing cells are tested by T-24; tie-breaking of `nx.top`, `Factor.top`, `rank("ordinal")` (the comparison uses `tiebreak = "id"`) [V31-29, R3-12].

| Stage | Comparison | Tolerance | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1-a price terms | `close.latest`, `Returns(2/21/252)`, `SMA(20/200)`, `EWMA`, `AverageDollarVolume(63)`, `AnnualizedVolatility`, `VWAP`, `RSI`, 3 user CustomFactors (12-1 momentum over 253 rows, SMA ratio, threshold Filter `close > X`) — **default inputs on both sides** (our default for return series = `close_tr` ↔ zipline `USEquityPricing.close`; windows that contain dividends included) | `|a−b| ≤ 1e-9 + 1e-9·|b|` | The threshold Filter tests the adjustment reference point (C2). Level factors (`SMA`, `VWAP`) are compared with the same input stated explicitly [RV-I02] |
| L1-b adjustment | windows of ±5 sessions before and after splits, reverse splits and dividends | same | `compat.zipline.USEquityPricing.close` ↔ `Price.close_tr` |
| L1-c point-in-time fundamentals | `Fund.x.latest` vs `EventsLoader` (facts without amendments, timestamp = available_at) | equal values | facts with amendments are compared against the reference producer's panel (M2) |
| L1-d masks and classification | `top(N, mask=)`, `rank(ordinal, groupby=)`, `zscore`, `percentile_between`, `quantiles` | sets and ranks exact | |
| L2-a backtest, zero costs | month-end top-N equal weight, preset zipline + `information_lag_sessions=1` (zipline's `before_trading_start` has data up to the previous day), sizing price = close of the decision day, fill at the next day's close, slippage 0, commission 0 | daily NAV relative 1e-8, fill quantities exact | |
| L2-b costs on | FixedBasisPointsSlippage(5, 0.1), PerShare(0.001), including cases where the cap binds | NAV relative 1e-6, quantities exact | the preset implements the 10% same-bar cap |
| L2-c corporate actions | holdings through a split, a cash dividend (pay date) and a delisting | valuation absolute 0.01 (currency) | delisting = last price (preset) |
| L2-e long–short (preset) [V31-LS-11] | month-end top-N long + bottom-N short equal weight, zero costs, zipline negative holdings | daily NAV relative 1e-8, fill quantities exact | windows that include short dividend payments |
| L2-d order types (preset semantics) | in the preset `zipline`, limit, stop and stop-limit buys and sells, day/GTC, partial-fill carry-over: trigger session, fill quantity, fill price | quantities and sessions exact, price relative 1e-9 | trigger = that bar's close alone (5.6.15, settled by T3.15b from the zipline code). Our OHLC rules are not tested here |
| L3 data round trip | export → bundle → zipline reader values | same (rounded inputs) | list of rows outside uint32 |

- **Evidence**: `tests/parity/evidence/<YYYYMMDD>-<sha>-<suite>-<fixture>.json` = `{suite, zipline_version, fixture_semantic_hash, cases: [{id, metric, n, max_abs, max_rel, tolerance, pass}], environment}`. Committed at the end of each milestone (like the reference producer's contract RESULTS) [design 4.8].
- Mixed markets (a sum of sleeves) have no zipline comparison. "The combined result = the sum of single-market runs (valued at exchange rates)" is checked by the property test M-SLEEVE (premise: `market_local`, no cross-market terms, 8.3) [design risk 13, SC-04, RV-C11].
- There is no second comparison engine (vectorbt or the like) (zipline is the only reference engine, 9.0 Q35). The canonical answer is the hand calculation `handcalc.py`.

### 8.2 Hand-calculated golden cases (`tests/golden/`) [11.8 M0, M3, M4]
- A case = `tests/golden/cases/<id>.yaml`: a description, a synthetic `tiny` event list (a table set by hand), the input (either a canonical target given directly to test execution only, or terms given to test the time rules), **expected values written out as literal numbers for each session and phase** (order events, fill quantity and price, costs (minor units), cash, holdings, `decision_value`, `nav_economic`, bridge rows).
- **No second engine** [RV-I05]: v2's "independent hand-calculation engine `handcalc.py`" meant writing the general state machine once more, so its errors correlate and it makes M0 heavy. In v3 the expected values are **per-session arithmetic for each case** (the numbers in the yaml + a check of a few lines next to the case file, `tests/golden/check/<id>.py`: arithmetic and rounding only, no loops, classes or engine imports). `tests/golden/test_handcalc_self.py::test_no_general_loops` checks via the AST that the check files have no `for`/`while`/`class` and do not import `nexusbt`. A person checks a few numbers of each case by hand and leaves them in a yaml comment (this is the truly independent verification).
- **Place and protection**: a case is written by another session in the **golden ticket right before** the implementation ticket that uses it (chapter 10, e.g. T3.Ga) and merged first. An implementation PR cannot change `tests/golden/cases/**` or `check/**` (`.github/CODEOWNERS` = the user + `tests/repo/test_golden_guard.py` rejects changes to those paths in the PR diff; if the spec is wrong, "spec deviation" + the golden ticket is reopened).

Time and Pipeline golden cases T-01~T-12: T-01 an amendment of an old period does not change the state of a new period, T-02 a future amendment does not change past values, T-03 the strict edition drops vendor lineage (present in compare), T-04 a month-end decision cannot use that month's panel, T-05 classification `known_at` late, T-06 the universe fallback rule, T-07 hand calculation of windows before and after splits and reverse splits, T-08 the `close_tr` window of a dividend whose amount comes late, T-09 symbol reuse (`instrument_symbols`), T-10 alignment of a start date that falls on a holiday, T-11 ties in `instrument_id` order (under `tiebreak = "id"`; the default hash is T-27) [R3-12], T-12 `Returns(232).shift(21)` = 253-row window `close[-22]/close[0]−1` = `P[t−21]/P[t−252]−1` [C7]. Added in v2: T-13 point-in-time lookup of `share_counts` and `shares_out`, `market_cap` after a split (5.3.5a), T-14 the two time axes of classification (effective but not yet known → the old classification), T-15 a `price_factor` null event makes the earlier rows of the window NaN (4.6), T-16 label `ts_label_start`, `ts_label_end` and purging at the end of a segment (5.8), T-17 with the `eligibility_daily` publication rule `session_close`, from that day's close decision on (5.1.5).

Added in v3.1: T-24 three-valued Filter — with close 12,000 / 8,000 / NaN (halted), `close > 10000` = true/false/unknown, `~(close > 10000)` = false/true/**unknown (finally false; true in v3)**, `(close > 10000) | close.isnan()` = true/false/true [V31-21]; v3.2: `(close > 10000).all(2)` is unknown for true and unknown, false if there is a false; unknown in `.as_factor()` → NaN; missing in `Meta.sector.eq("x")` → unknown; `(~Trad.halted).fillna(True)` [R3-13]; T-25 hand calculation of built-in factors (`tests/golden/factors/`): `SMA(3)` 1, NaN, 3 → 2.0; `EWMA(3, decay=0.5)` 1, 2, 3 → 2.428571; 1, NaN, 3 → NaN; `MaxDrawdown(4)` 10, 12, 9, 11 → 0.25; `Returns(3)` 100, NaN, 110 → 0.10; a large value 50 outside the window does not enter `MaxDrawdown(3)` (nautilus#4333) — 5-row cases with NaN positions for all 12 kinds [V31-24]; T-26 percentile composite — f1: A 10, B 20, C NaN, D 30 / f2: A 3, B 1, C 2, D NaN (higher is better for both): `pct_rank(na="drop")` f1 = 0, 50, NaN, 100; `"bottom"` f1 = 33.33, 66.67, 0, 100; `composite({f1: 1, f2: 1}, na="middle")` = A 50, B 0, C 50, D 100 [V31-25]; T-27 tie-breaking — `KR:T00001`, `T00002`, `T00003` with equal scores: `tiebreak="hash"` order T00002, T00001, T00003 (key = the first 8 bytes of `sha256("nexusbt-tiebreak-v1" ‖ 0x1F ‖ id)`: 2629704763309498885 < 4315078529452203467 < 8949987214584180479; the same at every decision and clock — v3.1's decision salt is dropped), `"id"` order T00001, T00002, T00003 [V31-29, R3-12]; T-28 market-level series — for a KR sleeve, `Market.series("IDX:KOSPI_TR").latest` is the index close at decision d; under `KR_close`, `Market.series("IDX:SPX_TR")` is the US close of d−1, and putting it in a window is E17 [V31-13].

Added in v3: T-18 rectangle lookup — a row announced in advance with a future effect does not hide the current row (5.3.6 examples 1~3), T-19 coexistence of classification levels and versions (examples 4 and 5), T-20 a late bar (`ts_end+PT5M`) and `ready_delay`: the decision uses that bar from the next row on (5.1.2), T-21 economic label 100 → dividend 10 → 100 = 10% (5.8), T-22 purging of a delisting label whose recovery is disclosed after the segment end (`ts_label_available`), T-23 the decision dates of `every(5)` do not depend on the start date (3.7).

The "known_at late" of `T-05` is the case where TDS `ts_known_from` is later than `effective_from`.

### 8.3 Property tests (`tests/property/`, hypothesis)

| id | property | generation |
|---|---|---|
| P-CHUNK | all node values bit-identical with chunk sizes of 1 year / 3 years / everything [INV-03] | synthetic small, random graphs |
| P-PERM | the same results with the instrument column order shuffled [INV-04] | |
| P-CACHE | cache hit = miss [INV-05] | |
| P-FUT | between a snapshot with the data after time τ deleted (rows with `ts_available > τ`: bars, event versions (`ts_known > τ`), delisting recoveries, FX, fundamentals) and the original snapshot, the targets and orders of decisions with `decision_ts ≤ τ` are the same; deleting an event version whose terms come late (an amount amendment) also leaves earlier decisions the same [INV-01, INV-02, R3-01] | random τ; includes the fixed case G-42 |
| P-ADJ | registered-kernel shortcut = reference (random events and scalars, relative 1e-12) [INV-11] | |
| P-NAV | accounting reconciles every session, money in integer minor units [INV-06] | random targets and events |
| P-BRIDGE | every session, the sum of the NAV and P&L bridge rows = the NAV change (difference 0) [INV-06]; it closes even with splits, dividends and distributions put at random on days without trades (halts) (V31-02), with ledger deposits and withdrawals (V31-19), with short and long–short holdings (borrow cost, short dividends) (V31-LS-08), and with cash interest and negative-cash obligations (R3-09, R3-25) | random targets, events, costs, flows, shorts |
| P-QTY | after every order and every event `requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`, no EXPIRED exit orders [INV-18]; exact ratios (`ratio_num/den` = 1/3, 1/7, 21/20, 129/100) applied to integer-multiple holdings give fractional-share cash 0 [V31-01]; the same for short holdings [V31-LS-05]; an event without an exact ratio is always E13 (with the literal setting, the decimal as it is, no inference), covers ≤ `|q|` [R3-10, R4F-01] | random orders, splits, OCO, shorts |
| P-RESUME | checkpoint and restart at a random session = in one go [INV-07, INV-10] | |
| P-PARITY13 | screen target = backtest target at 20 random decisions [INV-08] | |
| P-STATE | order state transitions stay within the table of 5.6.2 | |
| P-DET | the same input twice → the same physical hash of the output [INV-09]. v3.1: run in **two new processes** with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` [V31-08] | |
| P-STAT | PSR, DSR, PBO = independent test values (paper examples, hand-made matrices); bad inputs (N<2, T<2S) → n/a | |
| P-LIVE | no `fill` with sessions beyond the watermark; in the order **`live → fill → live` (same decision)** and in random orders, one order batch per decision, no duplicate fills [INV-19] | random call orders |
| P-BOOK | forced termination at every commit boundary of a ledger transaction (fragment write, checkpoint, HEAD, status) → reopening gives one valid state; two writers → one gets E35 [INV-19] | termination injected at each boundary |
| P-KERNEL | for each registered kernel, fast kernel = reference kernel (bits in the same environment), reference = the explicit left-fold definition (ADR-0019 A); for the transcendental class both paths use the same elementwise function [INV-13] | random windows and NaN positions |
| P-START | shifting the start date by 1, 21 or 252 sessions leaves the term values (registered-kernel bits) and canonical targets of the overlapping decisions the same (including `every(n)` — calendar anchor) [INV-14] | random graphs |
| P-DERIVED | `derive_tables(canonical events)` = the stored `positions`, `daily`, `metrics`, `attribution`, `exposures` [INV-16] | random strategies |
| P-RECON | after a paper ledger runs for n sessions, `reconcile` = yes; changing one ledger event gives the first difference at that date and phase [INV-10] | |
| P-FILLCAUSAL | (i) between the original snapshot and one where, at a random session t, volume, orders and the state before the phase are fixed and only that day's H, L, C (and all data after t) are changed, the quantity, `fill_price` and cost components (minor units) of open-phase fills (`at=open` market, limit and stop set at the open) are the same; intraday-phase fills are the same when only `C_t` is changed. (ii) For an **isolated order** with no other orders and ample cash, increasing `volume_t` does not decrease the fill quantity (monotone non-decreasing), the fill quantity is at most `floor_lot(η·ADV_t)` for every `volume_t`, and the fill price does not depend on `volume_t` (the total cost changes with the quantity, so it is not compared) [INV-01, V31-22, R3-03]. v3.1's "increasing `volume_t` does not increase the fill quantity" contradicted the second line of G-56 (up to 50,000 at 1,000,000), so a correct implementation failed it | random sessions and variants |
| P-SLEEVE-ISO | with targets fixed, replaying each sleeve separately = replaying them together (except cross-market tax payments), scenario column batches = separately [INV-21, V31-11] | random targets |
| P-GRID | every limit price, stop price, exit price and computed U, D is on that session's grid; adding ±1 ULP to bar prices leaves the fill results the same; the order of pairs across price-band boundaries is preserved in minimum-unit integers, and off-grid observed bars are not moved [INV-20, V31-05, R3-02, R3-14] | random prices and orders |
| P-SHORT-OFF | (i) **same-build differential**: running the example strategies and random long strategies with `[shorting] enabled = false` and with `enabled = true` + no negative targets + default exposure (`max_gross ≥ 1`, `max_net ⊇ [0, 1]`) gives the same value hash for every output table; (ii) 0 short events (`borrow_fee`, `short_rebate`, `dividend_short`), `side ∈ {sell_short, buy_to_cover}` and `reserved_minor ≠ 0` in an off run; (iii) **baseline**: the off run matches the **value hashes** (not physical bytes) of the four core tables (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `daily`) that T4.17 recorded before the short-sale tickets — the baseline files are a golden-protected path, updated only by a ticket that changes the schema, with a reason in the PR + the user-approval label `baseline-update`; (iv) the independent long golden cases G-01~G-66 pass [INV-22, V31-LS-09, R3-11]. v3.1's "all output bytes = a fixed hash file" was dropped because it collided with the legitimate schema and report changes of later tickets | example strategies, random long strategies |
| P-MARGIN-OFF | [R4G-MG-09] (i) **baseline**: the value hashes of the four core tables (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `daily`) of a `[account] type = "cash"` (default) run equal the baseline from before T4.20 (the same protected path and `baseline-update` label as P-SHORT-OFF); (ii) 0 `margin_interest`, `margin_call`, `forced_liquidation` events in a cash-account run, 0 negative cash after fills (except obligation `shortfall`); (iii) **same-build differential**: a long-only strategy with `type = "margin"` + `initial_margin = 1.0` + `debit_rate_bps = 0` + a `maintenance_margin` that never triggers has the same value hash for every output table as the cash account (buying power = E − R(1) = cash) | example strategies, random long strategies |
| P-TRADES | `Σ realised per trade + unrealised of open trades = period P&L` (costs, distributions, borrow included), a flipping fill is split into a closing share and an opening share, MAE and MFE use only prices after entry, without the extremes of the entry and exit bars [V31-20, R3-20] | random orders, splits, shorts |
| P-CACHE-INCR | on a new snapshot where only the last year partition changed, the result of L0 and L1 hits = computing without cache; with an amendment of a past partition or of an old event, misses from that year on [INV-05, V31-12]; v3.2 cases [R3-04]: the overlapping window of January rows sees an amendment of the previous year's partition, an amendment of a version in the middle of a chain, an amendment that changes only the publication time, amendments of `instruments` and `calendar_sessions`, a snapshot where only the watermark changed keeps its L0 hits, a wrong `value_sha256` (H004) does not reuse another block, a partial L1 cover does not fill missing rows — the whole result (hit vs miss) is compared, not the keys | random amendment positions |
| P-RISK-OFF | (i) between a run without account rules and a run with an account rule that is always m = 1 (`drawdown_brake(off=0.99, on=0.98)` on data where it does not trigger), the value hashes of all tables outside INV-23's exclusion list (`risk_state`, the `account:*` rows of `waterfall`, `graph_hash`, `settings_hash`, `run_id`, the report's "risk order" line) are the same [R4F-08]; (ii) without account rules, `final_weight ≡ target_weight` (bits) and the account layer is not called; (iii) `top(n, weight=w, max_weight=c)` = `top(n), sizing=nx.size.w(max_weight=c)` (bits); (iv) together with `[shorting] enabled = false`, the INV-22 baseline holds [INV-23, R4-04] | example and random long strategies |
| P-SIZE-PURE | sizers and market rules do not receive the account: randomly changing the account state (cash, holdings, NAV) leaves `target_weight` bit-identical; per side of the sizer output `Σ|w| ≤ 1`, after modifiers `Σ|w| ≤ min(1, max_gross)` and `Σw⁺ + c·Σ|w⁻| ≤ 1` [INV-08, R4-02, R4F-07] | random selections and windows |
| P-RISK-SHRINK | after every risk rule (built-in and random user rules) `|w_after| ≤ |w_before|`, the same sign, the reduced share goes to cash (no redistribution); bit-identical for the same order [R4-05] | random rule lists |
| P-WF-STITCH | for a strategy with nothing to choose (no `grid`, no ML), the stitched OOS series and all table values of `method="walk_forward"` are bit-identical to `backtest(start=t_1, method="linear")` (starting with capital K at the first test decision `t_1`) [R4-20, R4F-10] | example strategies |
| P-SPLIT-DET | the split list and `split_hash` are the same in two new processes; in no fold does training contain a sample caught by the purge condition (`max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` overlaps the test block) or by the embargo; CPCV path count = `k·C(N,k)/N` [R4-19] | random N, k, decision grids, late-disclosed labels |
| P-ML-REPRO | with `[ml] threads = 1`, `pred_sha256` is the same for each training in `rerun`; in every method (walk-forward, CPCV, segments) the training samples have no labels after τ (label availability time + embargo ≤ τ, forward only [R4F-03]); CPCV training samples have no decisions of that split's test blocks; shifting the start date leaves the training at the same τ the same (INV-14) [R4-23] | a deterministic toy model (a test linear regression with only `fit/predict`) |
| P-SKIP | per decision, `universe count = selected + skips (selection stage) + out of rank` and `target count = order intents + skips (execution stage) + suppression events` match exactly; 0 skips without a reason [R4-17] | random missing values and halts |
| P-TDS | the output of every preset and `degrade` combination of `make_snapshot` passes `tds check --level full` with the declared profile; the `capabilities` and `fallbacks` of a snapshot opened by the engine equal the tables of 4.3 and 4.5 [INV-17] | |

**Metamorphic tests** (`tests/property/test_metamorphic.py`) [SC-04, INV-15, RV-C11]. Each relation has a **premise**, and the test keeps both random graphs that satisfy the premise (cases where it holds) and fixed counterexamples that deliberately break the premise (confirming that the relation breaks):

| id | transformation | premise | relation |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-SCALE | all raw prices of one instrument ×k, volume ×1/k, factors unchanged | every term of the graph is homogeneous of degree 0 in that instrument (returns, ratios and their ranks), and there are no price-level thresholds, price-level ranks or costs (minimum commission) | degree-0 homogeneous terms, selection and target weights invariant, degree-1 homogeneous terms ×k. Counterexample: the threshold `close > X` |
| M-DUMMY | add an instrument that enters the universe at no decision | that instrument enters **the mask of no cross-sectional operation** of the graph (every cross-sectional operation has a mask no larger than the universe) | all results invariant. Counterexample: a universe on top of a `zscore` without a mask |
| M-SPLIT-NEUTRAL | add a fake split event with ratio 1.0 | – | all results invariant |
| M-CCY | `base_ccy` KRW ↔ USD in a strategy with only a US sleeve | no currency-unit terms in the decision inputs (no `.to(ccy)`) | USD-based returns the same, the KRW combined return = the USD return + the FX and cross terms of currency attribution (5.6.9) |
| M-SLEEVE | a combined KR+US run vs two single-sleeve runs | `clock = "market_local"`, no cross-market terms (`across_markets`, `.to(ccy)`) | combined NAV = the sum of the single NAVs valued at FX (currency minor units). Counterexample: `KR_close` + a cross-market rank |
| M-START | the same as P-START | target-state recursion off | |

### 8.4 Look-ahead and hygiene test list [R-51, R-52]
`tests/unit/test_determinism_rules.py` (v3.1): 0 wall-clock, random and `uuid4` calls in decision-path modules, 0 places where iteration over an unsorted `set` decides event order (AST) [V31-08]. `tests/unit/test_leaks.py`: negative shift (E1 static), **an injected future-reading source** (a test reader passes the whole-period array of the snapshot in through a closure, and a function reads values after the decision date → probe E1; `::test_probe_catches_injected_future_reader`), `np.roll(close, -5)` that wraps only inside the window (it does not read the future, so **E1 must not be raised**; `::test_bounded_roll_not_flagged`) [RV-C11], a function that uses a whole-period mean precomputed in a global (E24 + probe + L1 off), changing the constant global `THRESHOLD` (the cache key changes, no E24), `Label` input (E2), data without `known_on` (E3), an attempt to use that month's panel in a month-end decision (by construction, the value passed = the panel passed), a survivorship-biased list (E4), mixing currencies (E5), segment exposure records (E10), a function that uses state outside the window (global accumulation → E32 start-date probe), the priority of probe date selection (6.5 a/b/c), an attempt to use an `is_label` column like a `Panel` (E2), a `backfilled` dataset in a strict run (E30).

### 8.4a TDS conformance tests (`tds/tests/`, package `nexusbt-tds`)
- For each check code there are `tds/conformance/<code>/fail/` (a small package that raises only that code) and `pass/`, and `test_conformance.py::test_<code>` checks fail → exactly that code, pass → 0 errors (TDS 8.5). The packages are made by the generator script `tds/conformance/build.py`, which pins their hashes (no hand-edited files).
- `test_cli_exit_codes` (0/1/2/64/66/70), `test_jsonl_output_schema`, `test_value_hash_ignores_physical_choices` (changing compression, row group or dictionary gives the same value and semantic hashes), `test_partition_columns_from_path`, `test_vectors_match_published` (`tds/conformance/hash-vectors.json`; the test vectors are language-independent hand-written byte strings and their sha256), `test_availability_change_changes_semantic`, `test_profile_three_results` (declared / check level / not_checked), `test_M012_path_escape`, `test_M013_duplicate_path`, `test_M014_unknown_required_rule`, `test_experimental_schemas_flagged`.
- Engine side: `tests/unit/data/test_open_snapshot.py` checks that the E14 message contains the TDS code and (when present) the `producer.hints` sentence, and no producer ticket number [RV-I09].

### 8.4b Error layer tests (`tests/errors/`) [R4-15, R4-16]
- `layers.toml`: one layer per error code (+ condition id) (or two in order, such as `data → your code`). The same as the layer lines of the 6.6 table.
- `cases/<code>-<condition>.py`: the smallest code that raises that error (synthetic `tiny`). `test_every_code_has_layer_case` (every code and condition has a case), `test_layer_line_matches_table`, `test_layer_tail_fields` (the tail per layer: data = snapshot, dataset, column, producer; your code = file:line, function; config = table, key, value; engine bug = the `nexusbt bugreport` command), `test_fix_line_present`.
- `test_no_internal_terms.py`: 0 engine module or class names outside the public name table (3.1) in every case sentence of the layers other than engine bug.
- `test_user_exception_note.py`: `KeyError`, `IndexError` and the like inside a user function keep their original class and traceback, and get the layer and fix lines attached through `add_note` (R-42).

### 8.5 Documentation example tests [R-53, R-45]
The ```python blocks of README, `docs/` and `examples/` that carry the `<!-- test -->` mark are run by `tests/docs/test_examples.py` on the synthetic `small` snapshot. At each milestone, that milestone's examples (chapter 10 "commands to show") join this test. The **English quick start** (`docs/quickstart.md`) is a document that follows two CSVs (small hand-written files) → `tds import csv` → `tds check` → `evaluate` → a saved run → `rerun` without knowledge of the private producer, and `::test_quickstart_csv_to_rerun_en` runs it as it is [RV-I03].


- **Output compared too** [R4-13] (absorbed from ml4t, TD-ML4T-03): fails when the text of the `<!-- output -->` block next to a `<!-- test -->` block differs from the run output (fields that change, such as times and paths, are replaced by fixed placeholders).
- **Templates** [R4-13]: for each of the 22 templates of `nexusbt new strategy --template T` (3.10) (the test of each template is the acceptance test of the ticket that turns on its feature: `buffer-hold` T7.1, `walk-forward-cpcv` and `ml-factor` T6.27, `zipline-port` T5.19, the two event ones T3.35 [R4F-14]), `tests/docs/test_templates.py::test_template_runs[T]` runs `backtest --preview` and `screen` on the `init --demo` snapshot and compares them with the output blocks.
- **The 30-minute path** [R4-13, R4F-13]: `tests/docs/test_first_30_minutes.py` runs, in a clean virtual environment, the five commands `uv init quant && cd quant && uv add nexusbt` (the first line runs as one command even without a project; CI, before publishing on PyPI, uses `uv add nexusbt --find-links dist/` — the same clean wheel as the release gate T6.15) → `nexusbt init --demo` → `nexusbt new strategy momo --template monthly-topn` → `nexusbt backtest momo.py --preview` → `nexusbt screen momo.py` in turn, and checks that each command ends without error and that the total is under 2 minutes (CI runner). The 30 minutes a person measures is the pre-release user test (T5.18 Done means).
- **zipline → NexusBT mapping table** [R4-14]: the table of `docs/migrate-from-zipline.md` (English canonical + Korean) (what bundle, `initialize`, `handle_data`, `context`, `schedule_function`, `order_target_percent`, `attach_pipeline`, `pipeline_output`, `run_algorithm` and `CustomFactor` each become) and the `zipline-port` template, a runnable example with the same content; every code snippet in the NexusBT column of the table is `<!-- test -->`.
