## 9. The order of building with plinth (including the TDS conformance checker package) [R-65]

The engine is built with the user's plinth loop (`plinth/2.7.1/docs/how-to/run-a-project.md`). One rule of plinth — **every "done" is something a person can see and confirm directly** — is carried by every ticket of this spec as its "Done means" line.

| plinth step | In NexusBT | Who | This document |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Start | **after the name is settled** (OI-05) `/plinth:new-project <owner>/<name> --archetype=library` (**public repository**) → merge the first PR → token, account and the four manual steps → check `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0) and `NOTICE` | user | 9.1 |
| 2 Gather the thinking | `CONTEXT.md` (terms) and the first ADRs in one PR | agent writes, user merges | 9.2, 9.3 |
| 3 Plan the big work | put this spec into the repository as `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md` [R6-02] and TDS as `tds/spec/tds-v1.md` (both public editions without private comments, personal information and paths, 2.7), one plan issue with `/to-spec`, the chapter 10 tickets as issues with `/to-tickets` | user runs the commands | 9.4, chapter 10 |
| 4 Build one ticket | `/clear` → `/implement #N` (tdd + code-review), one ticket per session | user runs the commands, agent implements | 9.5 |
| 5 Confirm what the checks cannot see | required checks green + the ticket's "Done means" seen with one's own eyes through `/run` | user | 9.6, chapter 10 "commands to show" per milestone |
| 6 Merge | the user says "merge" | user | |
| 7 Breakage | `diagnosing-bugs`: reproduction command → failing test → fix | agent | 9.7 |
| 8 Stop and come back | state in issues, PRs and `docs/requirements.md`, `/handoff` | both | 9.8 |
| 9 Stay healthy | dependency updates one at a time, `/plinth:floor-check` | user | 9.8 |

### 9.1 Step 1: the repository and the required checks

**Create** (typed by the user):

```text
/plinth:new-project <owner>/nexusbt --archetype=library
```

- Create it **public** (user decision 2026-10-27, 2.1). plinth 2.7.1 `new-project.sh` defaults to public (`visibility=public`) and only a private repository takes `--private`, so there is no separate public flag [R7-09: local plinth 2.7.1 `scripts/new-project.sh` lines 5, 85 and 236 checked, 2026-10-29]. After creating it, check that `gh repo view <owner>/nexusbt --json visibility` is `PUBLIC`. Publishing before the name (9.0 N1) is settled leaves the repository name in the public record, so creating it after the name is settled is recommended (chapter 12 OI-05).
- The first PR adds `LICENSE` (the full Apache-2.0 text), `NOTICE`, `license = "Apache-2.0"` in `pyproject.toml`, and "engine and data standard are public; no data ships in this repository" in the repository description.

- `library` archetype: the container image check (`image`) is not required (the engine is not a service). The CLI is also an entry point of the same package (`[project.scripts] nexusbt = "nx.cli.main:main"`).
- Merge the first PR → `git switch main && git pull` → agent token (no workflow permission) → agent account → the four manual steps. Among them, "real data outside the agent session": the agent develops and tests only with synthetic TDS packages (4.8). Measurements that use the real export (`bench/`) and manual comparison runs happen only when the user runs them on the compute machine, and the committed evidence is summary figures only (2.7).

**The required checks plinth provides** (as they are; the points the engine must follow):

| Check | What the engine keeps | Local command |
|---|---|---|
| `ci / pr-title` | PR title = ticket title (Conventional Commits, not ending in `(#N)`) | – |
| `ci / lint` | ruff, `uv.lock` in agreement | `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check .` |
| `ci / typecheck` | mypy. `[tool.mypy] strict = true` (src); Numba functions get their types from the stub `nexusbt/compute/_jit.pyi` | `uv run mypy .` |
| `ci / test` | `uv run pytest` runs the **default markers** (unit, golden, property `ci` profile, docs, repository hygiene 2.7) (parity, perf_smoke, examples, slow and tds are separate jobs). Coverage also tests the `.py_func` of Numba kernels so that their lines are counted | `uv run pytest` |
| `ci / build` | wheel and sdist of the two distributions (`nexusbt`, `nexusbt-tds`); after install, `import nexusbt as nx`, `import nexusbt_tds`, `tds --help` | `uv build --all-packages` |
| `ci / secrets`, `ci / deps` | no secrets, no vulnerable dependencies. Because the repository is public, no personal paths or hosts either (`test_public_hygiene` of 2.7 blocks them separately) | – |
| `ci / diff-size` | one PR ≤ 400 lines (documents and lock excluded). **The basis of the ticket size rule** (chapter 10). `[tool.*]`, check settings and deleting tests need administrator approval | `git diff --stat main` |
| `ci / floor-check`, CodeQL | keep the template floor | `/plinth:floor-check` |

**Checks the engine adds** (jobs added next to the plinth-calling job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the agent token cannot change workflows, so the user pushes them or approves them with the user account — ticket T0.10; after they are added, the user puts them in the ruleset's required list):

| Check name | Content | Local command | Required from |
|---|---|---|---|
| `engine / parity` | L1 and L2 on synthetic small in the `tests/parity` environment (Python 3.12, zipline-reloaded 3.2.1) → the engine-side comparison pytest | `uv sync --project tests/parity --locked && uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite all --fixture small && uv run pytest -m parity` | end of M1 (L1), end of M3 (L2) |
| `engine / property-deep` | hypothesis `deep` profile (e.g. 500 cases) | `uv run pytest -m property --hypothesis-profile=deep` | M1 |
| `engine / perf-smoke` | the CI performance smoke tests of chapter 7 | `uv run pytest -m perf_smoke` | M3 |
| `engine / docs-examples` | 8.5 (also in the default `ci / test`, but the full run of `examples/` is here) | `uv run pytest tests/docs -m examples` | M2 |
| `tds / conformance` | the own tests of the **TDS conformance checker package** (`tds/`) (8.4a: pass and fail packages per code), and `tds check --level full` on synthetic `tiny` and `small` and every `degrade` combination | `uv run --package nexusbt-tds pytest tds/tests && uv run python -m nexusbt.testing.synth --preset small --out .scratch/snap --validate` | M0 (end of T0.14c; the `full` level at the end of M1) |
| `tds / schemas` | `tds/schemas/**.json` and the TDS document tables are the same (the document tables are generated from the schema files; this catches hand-edited differences); checks for violations of the TDS version rules (10.2): no added required columns or type changes within the same MAJOR, only `stable` schemas carry a compatibility promise | `uv run --package nexusbt-tds python -m nexusbt_tds.schemas.verify` | M0 |
| `engine / golden-guard` | implementation PRs do not change `tests/golden/cases/**` or `check/**` (allowed only for golden ticket PRs titled `test(golden): …`) [RV-I05] | `uv run pytest tests/repo/test_golden_guard.py` | M0 |
| `engine / release` | install only the two wheels in a clean virtual environment (outside the working folder) and run the English quick start and the public examples; the independent producer test package (`tds/conformance/producers/csv-minimal`) passes `full` [RV-I07] | `uv run python -m tests.repo.release_check` | M6 |

Manual on the compute machine (not a required check; summary evidence committed at the end of a milestone): full performance `uv run python bench/run.py --all --snapshot <id>`, real-data comparison `uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite all --fixture real:<id>`.

pytest markers: `unit`, `golden`, `property`, `docs`, `examples`, `parity`, `perf_smoke`, `slow`, `repo`.

**Operating the TDS package** (versioned separately): `tds/` is a uv workspace member in the same repository as the engine, but its version, change log (`tds/CHANGELOG.md`) and tags are separate. The release states are **draft → RC → 1.0** (tags `tds-v1.0.0-rc.N` → `tds-v1.0.0`; the `tds_version` string stays `MAJOR.MINOR`, `"1.0"`). Conditions for graduating to 1.0 (TDS 10.5): one independent producer (it may go through the import tool) and one producer export pass `full`, the engine reads both and the same example runs, and the hash test vectors agree between the two implementations [RV-I07]. PRs that change the TDS documents, schemas or checker use the title scope `tds` (e.g. `feat(tds): …`) and raise `tds_version` according to the MINOR/MAJOR rules of TDS 10.2. The engine writes the version range of `nexusbt-tds` (`>=1.0,<2`) as its dependency. The reference producer also runs the `full` check right after export with the same package (`pip install nexusbt-tds` or a repository path). In `pyproject.toml`, `addopts = "-m 'not parity and not perf_smoke and not examples and not slow' --hypothesis-profile=ci"`. Private comment: the reference producer also runs the `full` check right after export with the same package.

### 9.2 Step 2: the whole `CONTEXT.md` (proposed content, put in as it is)

```markdown
# NexusBT glossary (CONTEXT.md)

In this repository the words below mean exactly this. Code names are in English, in parentheses.

## Five nouns the user knows
- **Data column** (`Price.close`, `Fund.equity`, `Meta.sector`, `Panel.bm`, `Label.*`): an importable attribute built from the snapshot's column dictionary. `Label.*` is a label (ground truth), so it is used only in `evaluate(target=…)`.
- **Term** (`Factor`, `Filter`, `Classifier`): a node of the computation graph, built from columns and combined with other terms. Same names and meanings as the zipline Pipeline.
- **Strategy** (`Strategy`): universe + signal + schedule + portfolio rules + execution, exits, costs. A value object.
- **Run**: one call of evaluate/backtest/screen/sweep/live. A folder sealed into `runs/<run_id>/`.
- **Config** (`nexusbt.toml`): project defaults. Data location, edition, base currency, reference clock, segments, costs.

## Data and time
- **TDS** (NexusBT Data Standard): the only input format the engine reads. A folder + `tds.json` + Parquet. Versioned separately under `tds/` of this repository. Checked by `tds check`.
- **Producer**: whoever builds a TDS package (an individual's CSV import, a data warehouse's export, …). The engine reads packages only and knows no particular producer. Producer hints go only in `tds.json producer.hints`.
- **Instrument id** (`instrument_id`): an opaque, never-reused string key chosen by the producer. No aliases.
- **Snapshot / package**: a published, immutable TDS folder. A `snapshot_id` and three hashes (physical, value, semantic). The engine decides "same data" by the semantic hash.
- **Profile**: a TDS bundle a package declares it follows (`@version`). A conformance claim only; what the engine needs is the data for the features the graph uses (capability). Results come in three: declared / check level / supported.
- **Resolver**: the function that, given a reference time, resolves the amendments, cancellations and terms disclosures of corporate actions into a price factor, a share factor, entitlement and settlement. Adjustment, accounting and checks all use it.
- **Fallback**: a defined engine behaviour when a profile or column is missing. Always a warning (E30) and a manifest record. Never a hidden fallback.
- **Edition**: `strict` (the strict edition, default; only values whose lineage has no vendor input or after-the-fact publication) / `compare` (vendor values included, for comparison).
- **Market**: a key of TDS `markets` (for example `KR` = XKRX/KRW, `US` = XNYS/USD). Never hard-coded. **Supported market** = a market with a market rules pack (`resources/markets/<M>.toml`).
- **Session**: one trading day of a market calendar. `ts_open` and `ts_close` are the real instants; the date is a label.
- **Availability time** (`ts_available`): the first UTC instant the observation may be used. A date-only value becomes usable at the next session open.
- **Information cutoff** (`decision_ts`): the last instant of information a decision may use. One rule: `ts_available <= decision_ts`.
- **As of**: always means "as of decision_ts". Currencies and date strings never set the as-of time.
- **Market axis**: the session sequence of one market. Row s freezes at the freeze time `ready(s) = ts_close(s) + declared delay` and is not changed by data that arrives afterwards.
- **Phase**: the order inside a session: `session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end`. Equal instants follow this order.
- **As-of adjustment**: past prices in a window adjusted only by splits and dividends up to the computation day. Future splits are unknown.
- **fundamentals_state**: financial-state intervals `[ts_known_from, ts_known_to)` that the producer resolved (period choice, TTM, source priority, strict eligibility). The engine only looks the intervals up. The raw facts are `fundamentals_events` (for explanation).
- **Two time axes**: classifications and index membership are used only when both the effective period (`effective_from/to`) and the known period (`ts_known_from/to`) hold.
- **Forward cumulative factors** (`cum_factor_split/total`): products over events starting at 1.0 on the first listed session. New data never changes old values.
- **Watermark**: the last session per market at which the snapshot is complete. live never uses anything after it for fills.

## Decisions and accounts
- **Sleeve**: an account compartment funded separately for one market. Funded once at the start; no transfers between sleeves afterwards. Target weights sum to at most 1 within a sleeve.
- **Reference clock** (`clock`): the decision time for multi-market strategies. `"<MARKET>_close"` (for example `KR_close`: Korean close, US values from the previous day), `market_local`.
- **Base currency** (`base_ccy`): the currency of combined reports. Changes valuation only.
- **Decision** (`DecisionContext`): one time a sleeve sets its targets. Records id, decision_ts, session and snapshot.
- **Eligibility / tradability**: eligibility = can it be chosen at the decision (universe). Tradability = can it fill in the execution session (state machine).
- **Canonical target**: the decision's `(instrument_id, target_weight)` table. The result of a pure function independent of the account state. What must be identical in screen and backtest. Two stages: construction (`portfolio=`) → overlay (`risk=`).
- **Target state / anchor**: the previous decision's canonical target set and the decision where that recursion started. The hold band (`hold=`, the last v1 ticket T7.1) relies on it instead of account holdings [R6-12].
- **Order intent**: a fixed share quantity order computed from the canonical target + the account state.
- **Order status**: NEW → ACCEPTED → (TRIGGERED) → PARTIALLY_FILLED → FILLED, or CANCELLED / EXPIRED / REJECTED. A trigger is a separately persisting state, and quantities always satisfy `requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`. "Blocked" is a session event, not a status.
- **Exit order**: a protective sell order that never expires, created right after the entry fill and resized on every holding change. Cancelled only when the holding is zero.
- **Order types**: market, limit, stop, stop-limit. Time in force day / gtc.
- **Adverse assumption**: an unknown price order inside a daily bar is assumed adverse to the order owner. Short sales and covers mirror the same rule.
- **Signed position**: long = positive, short = negative. Shorting is off by default; when on, collateral, borrow cost and short dividend payments come with it.
- **Price grid**: the tick size of that market, date and price band. Order prices snap conservatively to the grid, and comparisons use integer market minimum units (not tick counts that differ by price band).
- **Three-valued Filter**: true, false, unknown. A missing input gives unknown; at the final use, unknown is false. Replacement only via `Filter.fillna(bool)`.
- **Ruined sleeve** (`ruined`): the state where the decision value of a sleeve with shorts is 0 or below. New orders stop, and only mandatory obligations are booked, as negative cash. The cash account has no forced liquidation (the margin account liquidates fully when equity is 0 or below, 5.6.7a) [R6-07].
- **Participation cap**: daily fill ≤ maximum participation × average volume of the past 20 sessions. The rest carries over.
- **decision_value / nav_economic**: the account value used for decision sizing (only what is known at the decision time; entitlements to dividends and delisting recoveries of unknown amount excluded) / the economic value used for reports (later amounts included).
- **Money**: booked in integer minor units (KRW 1 won, USD 1 cent), rounded once per entry. The NAV and P&L bridge closes every day.
- **Entitlement**: a right to receive, created by holding at the ex-date. A receivable is an entitlement whose amount is known; cash arrives on the pay date.
- **Terminal value**: what a delisted holding receives. Three bases — observed-inclusive, observed-exclusive, policy (5.6.13) — applied once, together with any distribution just before delisting.
- **Provisional costs**: fixed bp costs used until cost tables are verified.
- **Fill record**: per fill, the decision resolution (`fill_resolution`), the rule (`fill_rule`), decision price, arrival price.
- **Event truth**: `orders` and `account_events` are the source of truth; the other tables are derived from them.
- **Attribution**: a table splitting returns into local, FX and cross terms and into cost components. The **exposure table** (`exposures.parquet`) is holdings exposure, not segment "exposure".
- **Reconcile**: comparing a paper ledger with a replay of the same snapshots to find the first difference. The result also goes in `status.json`.

## Research hygiene
- **Segment**: a user-named period (train/validation/test). Not locked; exposure is recorded.
- **Exposure**: the record of which segments a run computed performance on.
- **Study / `study_id`**: the group in which attempts are counted. Default name = file name; stored as a UUID.
- **Candidate / observation / cohort**: candidate = the identity of a distinct (graph, parameters, config, segment) inside a study; observation = statistics of one run; cohort = a collection of comparable observations. DSR's N and BHY's m are counted within a cohort.
- **Attempt counts**: runs / candidates / assumed independent trials are counted separately.
- **PSR, DSR, PBO**: probabilistic Sharpe, Sharpe deflated by attempts, probability of overfitting (CSCV). Shown only, never blocking.
- **Structural guarantee vs diagnostic**: time rules for engine-managed inputs are guaranteed by construction. Truncation and start-date probes of user functions are diagnostics only, not proofs.
- **Registered kernel and numeric order**: engine kernels compute in a defined summation order (windows by row, cross-sections by `instrument_id`), so bits are the same regardless of chunking and ordering.
- **MinTRL, BHY, naive breadth and expected IR, signal–weight correlation**: the minimum period needed to trust this Sharpe; multiple-testing adjusted p; naive opportunity count and IC·√breadth (not the number of independent bets); how much of the signal moved into the weights (no risk adjustment). Shown only.
- **Label**: default `fwd_return` = holding return with dividends reinvested at the ex-date close; `fwd_return_adj` = adjusted-price ratio, for comparison. Three label times (start, end, knowable time) purge labels at segment ends.
- **Oracle**: hand-written per-session arithmetic (golden), slow reference implementations, producer-resolved outputs, a pinned zipline-reloaded 3.2.1 (through an imitation preset only).
- **Block / slot**: a value plugged into one of the strategy's nine slots (schedule, universe, signal, construction, sizing, risk, execution, exits, costs). Each slot has fixed inputs, outputs and what it may read.
- **Sizing slot** (sizing): a pure function that sets weights for the chosen names. Never reads the account.
- **Risk slot** (risk): a list of rules that only scale weights toward zero. Rules that read the account (`reads_account=True`) run only in the **final target** layer.
- **Canonical target / final target** (`target_weight` / `final_weight`): canonical = a target from market information only (screen = backtest); final = the canonical target times the multipliers of account rules (the value orders use).
- **Shadow index**: the index of the canonical target held without costs. The drawdown brake uses it to decide when to re-enter.
- **Validation method / splitter / path** (method / splitter / path): the value of `backtest(method=…)`. Folds and paths are diagnostics and are not counted as attempts. Purge = drop training samples whose label interval (up to availability) overlaps the test block; embargo = drop training samples after the test block.
- **ML factor** (`ml.factor`): a Pipeline node for which the engine enforces the training window and that predicts at every decision. Model objects are not saved; prediction and fit tables are kept.
- **Error layer** (layer): data / your code / config / engine bug — the second line of an error.
```

### 9.3 Step 2: the ADRs to record first (`docs/adr/`, drafts)

| # | Title | Draft (one paragraph) |
|---|---|---|
| 0001 | Keep the words of the zipline Pipeline and build a new executor | Factor/Filter/Classifier, `.latest/.rank/.zscore/.top`, `mask=` and `groupby=` keep zipline's names and meanings. The slowness came not from the concepts but from per-date Python loops, window copies, per-holding value reads and bundle conversion (E0 measurement 20~68 times). So a planning and pruning executor over dense market-axis blocks is built anew. Cost: full zipline source compatibility is not offered, only the thin layer `compat.zipline`. [D1, D2] |
| 0002 | Read TDS packages without bundles, and use the stable identifier `instrument_id` | The user-side ingest step is removed. The engine reads TDS packages (Parquet + `tds.json`) as they are and builds integer indexes in memory. Renumbering sids per snapshot prevents fragment reuse and contradicts the hard-link promise (Codex C9), so files hold only `instrument_id`. The name is a single asset-neutral `instrument_id` with no aliases (three-reviewer consensus Q01; external names only in import mappings and `rename=`). Cost: a dictionary-index mapping cost at read time (to be confirmed by measurement). [D4, C9, TDS-D1, RV-Q01] |
| 0003 | Adjustment by cumulative factors + a reference implementation + shortcuts only for proven kernels | `P_t[s] = raw[s]·C[t]/C[s]` is exact, but an arbitrary term cannot be fixed by multiplying by a scalar (thresholds, nonlinearity, price-level ranks). So the slow reference implementation that builds as-of windows is the answer, and only registered kernels that declare a homogeneity class and pass the P-ADJ test use the shortcut. Because of reverse splits, event↔factor reconciliation is used instead of a monotonicity check. [D3, C2] |
| 0004 | Fundamentals are resolved by the producer (the reference producer); the engine only looks up TDS `fundamentals_state` | "The most recent filing" and "the latest amendment of the most recent period" are different queries, entangled with consolidated/separate statements, TTM and source choice. Building the accounting rules in two places makes them drift apart. As the user decided (9.0), the reference producer exports resolved intervals and the engine only looks up intervals. Cost: more work on the reference producer side, and until then tests use synthetic data only. [9.0 Q24, C3] |
| 0005 | One availability rule, `decision_ts`, reference clock, sleeves | Every observation is used only when `ts_available <= decision_ts`. Korea and the US are separately funded sleeves, and the reference clock (`KR_close` default, `US_close`, `market_local`) stays a setting (user decision). The base currency does not change information time. Cross-sectional operations are per market by default; cross-sections across markets only at the final stage. [9.0 Q23, C1, A-03] |
| 0006 | Put the daily state machine and the order types in v1 | By user decision, market, limit, stop and stop-limit orders, day/GTC, volume-cap partial fills and pending orders are implemented in v1 (Codex suggested postponing them). A daily bar tells only that O comes first and C last, not the order of H/L, so the adverse side is assumed. Intraday order only when minute or tick data exists (E4). Capacity is approximated by the average volume of the past 20 sessions and is not called a measure of opening liquidity. [9.0 orders, C4] |
| 0007 | Account P&L from raw prices and explicit events | Adjusted-price P&L and dividends are not mixed. Entitlement (ex-date), receivable (amount disclosed), cash (pay date) and valuation are kept apart. Dividends and delisting recoveries of unknown amount are left out of the decision value and put into the reporting value at the later amount (decision sizing inputs too, INV-01). Terminal value = reference price × (1+policy), once only. If an unsupported event happens to a holding, the run stops. [C5, 11.4.3, RV-C01] |
| 0008 | Costs are cash deductions; provisional bp before verification | Putting spread and impact into both price and cash double-counts, so every cost is recorded as a cash deduction. Until historical tax and commission tables are verified from sources, `provisional` fixed bp is the default and is marked in the report. Comparison at 0, 1 and 2 times costs always. [9.0, Q6, R-61] |
| 0009 | v1 is float64 only | float32 error can flip the top-N boundary. Memory P7 is remeasured with float64. float32 only as an explicit optimisation after measurement. [9.3 Q25, C7] |
| 0010 | Segments + attempt records + DSR display instead of locking the future | Future data accumulates too slowly, so nothing is locked (user decision). The user sets train/validation/test, and the engine records exposure and candidate counts and shows DSR and PBO. Run counts and candidate counts are kept apart, and without inputs the statistics are "n/a". [9.0 Q26, Q19, 9.3 Q27] |
| 0011 | zipline-reloaded 3.2.1 only as an isolated comparison reference | Everyday backtests use NexusBT. The pinned zipline runs only in the separate environment of `tests/parity`, and engine code does not import it. Known zipline bugs are not the answer. This decision replaces the user memory "backtest with zipline-reloaded". [9.0 Q35, D7] |
| 0012 | Run records: temporary → sealed, code saved, physical/value hashes, SQLite index | Source, lock and parameters are captured so that results can be made again, and sealing happens only after success (interruptions are recovered). File integrity (physical) and result identity (value, TDS 4.5 canonical serialisation) are kept apart. Attempt and exposure records are append-only and kept in SQLite, where transactions are simple. Without the snapshot, rerun fails. [C9, 11.7] |
| 0013 | Microcaps included by default | No hidden exclusions. One line `exclude_microcap = true` drops the bottom 20% by market cap per market. [9.0 Q31, Q17] |
| 0014 | Benchmarks default to KOSPI and S&P 500 with dividends; none is built when missing | Index series are read from the snapshot. When missing, "n/a", and no cap-weighted index of the investable names is built silently (that would be another strategy). [9.0 Q30] |
| 0015 | Missing dividend pay dates are estimated + marked | Estimated with the median of historical observed intervals per country and kind (the configured value when there are none), and counted as "estimated". `fail` can also be chosen. [9.0 Q29] |
| 0016 | Kernels in Python, numpy and Numba, with a seam where Rust can be swapped in [user decision 2026-10-28, R3-26] | Reference implementation (Python/numpy) → only measured hot loops in Numba. The three hot kernels (the window and cross-section kernels `nx.compute._kernels`, one step of the session simulator `nx.execution._step`, the accounting state `nx.accounting._state`) sit behind a narrow interface that exchanges only Arrow arrays (or numpy sharing their buffers) as input and output and columnar array state (2.4). Only when M1 and P1~P7 measurements and profiles show that Numba cannot meet the budget is that kernel replaced by a Rust crate (PyO3/maturin, optional build), together with a same-environment bit-identity test against the Python reference (`test_kernel_swap_bit_equal`). Nothing is rewritten before a profile finds the bottleneck. For E4 intraday and tick (minute and tick event handling, order book state), a Rust core is designed and then compared with the Nautilus bridge (11.2) to choose between them. Cost: Python objects cannot pass through the seam's signature (arrays and integer codes instead of callbacks and dicts). [Q14, Q-E4] |
| 0017 | Event engines as a boundary (Protocol) only, no `engine="auto"` | (v3.1: the backend only emits fill events, and the engine owns the accounting truth [V31-09]) NautilusTrader is attached behind `ExecutionBackend` in E4. v1 fails explicitly on unsupported features and does not silently change fill semantics. [D6, C10, R-48] |
| 0018 | The input contract is the public standard TDS v1; the checker is a separately versioned package | The engine and data preparation are separated by one format, and anyone's data is read by the same rules. The TDS documents and `tds/schemas` are canonical for the format, check codes and profiles, and the checker `nexusbt-tds` (import `nexusbt_tds`, pyarrow only) is shared by the engine and every producer. Spec chapter 4 holds only the engine-side reading rules. A profile is a conformance claim; what the engine requires is the graph's capability. Status draft → RC → 1.0; tick and derivatives schemas experimental. Cost: the work of managing standard versions (MAJOR/MINOR). [SC-24, R-66, TDS K1~K12, RV-I01, RV-I07] |
| 0019 | Numeric determinism: a defined summation order, sorted-index reductions, a same-environment promise | Keeping chunk and permutation invariance bit for bit needs the kernels' summation order to be part of the definition. The promise covers **the same environment** (same lock, same CPU kind). Windows are left folds in ascending row order (definition A), cross-sections are gathered in `instrument_id` sort order and folded (instead of an exact-sum alternative; faster), and cumulative-sum differences, O(1) sum updates, running sums, BLAS and fastmath are forbidden. Transcendental functions use the same compiled elementwise function on the reference and fast paths. The pre-registered alternative definition B (partial sums by absolute `session_ord // 64` blocks) is adopted by amending this ADR if definition A exceeds the budget in the M1 measurement. User functions only promise "same window → same output". Cost: some fast kernels get slower (measured in M1). [SC-03, OQ-5, RV-C11, RV-D1] |
| 0020 | The risk stage slot and the target-state recursion (hold band; v3.3: the detailed rules of the slot moved to ADR-0038, and this ADR keeps only the hold band `prior` = canonical target [R4-04]; v3.2: `TargetState = (S_long, S_short, missing_streak)`, plumbing in T5.16 [R3-22]) | A `risk=` slot with LEAN-style stage separation (until v3.1 "v1 has only `none`"; from v3.3 the v1 built-in rule list and the account layer — ADR-0038 [R6-06]). The hold band is defined to rely on the previous decision's canonical target set instead of account holdings, keeping the pure function and screen=backtest (anchor included). The norms now, the implementation in the last v1 M6 ticket T7.1 (user decision), off by default. v3.1: missing scores `on_missing`, hold-band names never bought are not bought at the execution stage (`band_entries`), the `bottom(hold=)` mirror, fitting the exposure caps of shorting and long–short is a mechanical step of the risk stage [V31-17, V31-LS-03]. [SC-01, SC-02, OQ-1, OQ-2, Q6] |
| 0021 | Narrow the zipline comparison scope to equal meaning | (v3.1: when the comparison differs, the hand calculation decides; when zipline is wrong, it is a known difference [V31-24]; long–short L2-e added [V31-LS-11]) zipline's daily-bar limit and stop triggering differs in meaning from our OHLC adverse-side rules [est.; the three-reviewer review confirmed the same direction upstream, the check on the pinned version is T3.15b]. Order types are compared only through a preset that imitates zipline's semantics, and our rules are tested with hand-written per-session arithmetic. Price terms are compared on default inputs on both sides (default for return series = `close_tr`). The "comparison test with zipline" of the user decision is kept within this scope (recommended by the three reviews, settled by user decision 2026-10-28 — OI-20 [R6-03]). [SC-12, A-57, RV-I02] |
| 0022 | Public repository, Apache-2.0, data outside the repository | The engine, the spec and TDS are public, real data is private (user decision). Apache-2.0 is the same as zipline-reloaded and compatible with the core dependencies; Nautilus (LGPL) only as an optional dependency. Repository hygiene tests block personal paths, data and vendor-derived files, and tests use only the synthetic generator. [user decision 2026-10-27, TDS-Q5] |
| 0023 | No pickle, secrets outside the config, other people's run folders only after a trust mark | Outputs, caches and checkpoints are Parquet, Arrow IPC and JSON only. key/secret/token/password keys are forbidden in `nexusbt.toml`. `rerun` executes source, so a run folder from another machine needs `--trust`. [SC-27, Qlib warning] |
| 0024 | A defined fallback behaviour when data is missing | So that the engine stays useful even when a valid package does not fill every column and dataset, a fallback table (F-01~F-14) is defined, always announced and always recorded. Packages that break their declarations are not fixed (that is the import step's job). When a result cannot be defined, the run fails. It can be turned off with `[data] fallbacks = "fail"`. Error sentences are producer-neutral. [R-67, RV-I01, RV-I09] |
| 0025 | A three-layer cache (L0 raw blocks, L1 term values, L2 runs) | To avoid Parquet decompression and dictionary-index conversion every time, a raw-block cache in uncompressed Arrow IPC is opened by memory mapping. The key includes the partition file sha256, every version and publication time of the corporate-action chains, the axis datasets, the stable `markets` fields (watermark excluded), the propagated input rows, the resolved query policy, external inputs and captured constant globals, so that it cannot drift (nodes that read non-constant globals turn L1 off; the optional `value_sha256` is not used as identity; L0 has an LRU separate from snapshot deletion) [R3-04]. Key rules in M1, implementation in M6. P7 also looks at the USS of the process tree. [SC-15, RV-C10] |
| 0026 | Events are the canonical output; attribution, exposure and reconciliation are derived | Every table must be rebuildable from `orders` and `account_events` (versioned schemas) (tested). Currency attribution is exact in local, FX and cross terms (annual by a defined linking), cost attribution as direct costs and path effects. Paper-ledger reconciliation is a replay of the same state machine with the recorded snapshots; comparison with the latest data is a restatement. [SC-07, SC-14, INV-16, RV-C07, RV-C13] |
| 0027 | One corporate-action resolver | If adjustment, accounting, labels and checks each resolve corporate-action amendments, cancellations and terms disclosures on their own, they drift apart. One pure function that takes a reference time gives one version per chain and four effects (price, share, entitlement, settlement), and makes correction entries when an amount already booked changes. Quantities through the explicit `share_factor`. Cost: calling the resolver at each reference time (negligible since chains are few [est.]). [RV-C03] |
| 0028 | Three hashes: physical, value, semantic | Arrow IPC bytes differ by implementation and version, so they cannot be a public standard's definition of "same data". A language-independent canonical value serialisation (with published test vectors) gives the value hash, the value hash + resolved metadata gives the semantic hash, and the engine uses the semantic hash. Cost: one serialisation function in the checker. [RV-C10] |
| 0029 | Market names from the data, market rules in packs | If a public engine hard-codes `KR` and `US`, the public API breaks for the first user of another market. Names come from `tds.json`, per-market rules from `resources/markets/<M>.toml`. Supported market = a market with a pack (v1 KR, US); the others E18 or `allow_untested` (recorded). [RV-I08] |
| 0030 | Money in integer minor units, accounting reconciled by the NAV and P&L bridge | float rounding is not exact minor-unit accounting, and "sum of cash flows = NAV change" misses valuation gains. Integer once per entry, reconciled daily by the bridge. gross − net = direct cost + path effect. [RV-C07] |
| 0031 | Order lifecycle: persistent triggers, quantity conservation, exit orders | Lifecycle state, trigger state and quantity accounting are kept apart, and exit orders are created right after the entry fill, resized on every holding change, and never expire. Intraday sale proceeds are not used for intraday buys in the same bar, and the buyable quantity uses the real cost function. The state machine in columnar arrays (Numba after measurement). [RV-C05, RV-C06, RV-I10] |
| 0032 | Paper-ledger transactions and idempotency | The idempotency key is (ledger, decision, graph, parameters), without the checkpoint number. A single-writer lock, state version comparison, the HEAD swap as the commit point, recovery at every commit boundary. Reconciliation defaults to a replay of the recorded snapshots; comparison with the latest data is a restatement. [RV-C13] |
| 0034 | Share counts in integer arithmetic; stale valuation prices match the event basis [V31-01, V31-02, V31-LS-05] | Float flooring turns one share into fractional-share cash for integer-multiple holdings (LEAN#9882, zipline-reloaded#336), and stale prices after an event on a day without trades inflate NAV and order sizes (LEAN#9819). Rational ratios (`ratio_num/ratio_den`) and `floor_shares`, and stale valuation price × the later price factor, are the norm. Shorts (negative quantities) follow the same rule. Exact ratios are required, and missing or wrong ones are E13; there is no f64 inference — the only exception is the decimal literal the user chose (recorded, warned) [R3-06 → R4F-01]. Cost: external data without exact ratios does not open until the user explicitly chooses |
| 0035 | The price grid and rounding rules in the market rules pack (in the data, when present) [V31-05] | Order prices snap conservatively to the grid and judgement uses integer market minimum units (not tick counts that differ by price band [R3-02]); money is rounded per component by pack rules (KR pack: commission, tax and withholding `down`, the rest half_up, user overrides [R3-27]). Source order: instrument → `market_rules` → pack → minimum unit. Cost: the tick-table seeds are unverified |
| 0036 | Shorting and long–short as the signed symmetry of the same state machine, off by default [V31-LS-01] | By user decision (2026-10-28) this changes 9.0 Q2 "long only". Holdings, weights, fill rules, exits, corporate actions and taxes mirror the long side, and only collateral, borrow cost and short dividends are short-specific items. Off gives the same bits (INV-22). Borrowable quantity, the uptick rule and forced liquidation (of the cash account) are not simulated and remain assumption sentences (forced liquidation of the margin account is 5.6.7a [R6-07]). v3.2 [R3-09, R3-10]: accrual at session start on the previous night's exposure, the rebate on the valued short, mandatory obligations as negative cash, short sleeve ruin `ruined` (new orders stop, metrics n/a), short obligation estimates replaced by the later amount, risk-reducing order in all three fill phases, the opening side of a reversal after the closing side, no margin for longs (feasible region E16) |
| 0037 | Snapshot immutability: cloning first, read-only, atomic publishing, file identity [user decision 2026-10-28, R3-16] | Producers do not modify published snapshot files in place. Unchanged partitions are reused by APFS cloning (`clonefile`) or reflink (changing one does not change another snapshot with it), and hard links + read-only only on file systems that cannot clone. Publishing = staging folder → check → atomic `LATEST`. The engine writes the identity `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)` of files whose sha256 it checked into stamps and L0, and recomputes when it differs. Pin default `index+root`; `retention: unmanaged` when that cannot be used. Cost: the identity check is not a cryptographic proof (`verify="full"` looks at every byte). [V31-PUB-01, R3-17] |
| 0038 | The sizing and risk slots and the account-reading layer [R4-01~R4-06] | Size is set by a pure-function slot (sizing) that does not read the account (sizing from the account, like backtrader or Qlib, gives screen ≠ backtest). Risk is a list slot that only outputs multipliers toward zero, and rules that read the account run only in the separate final-target layer after the canonical target (INV-08 as is, INV-23 new). State only in an engine table (`risk_state`); iterative solvers with a fixed order and iteration count, a defined fallback and count reporting; drawdown brake re-entry by the shadow index. The high-dimensional performance (P11) gate is written into this ADR after prototype measurement |
| 0039 | Validation is one `backtest(method=…)`; the ML factor is a training node inside the engine [R4-19~R4-24, user decision 2026-10-28] | One entry point (no separate `validate`), the linear result and the distribution side by side. Splits by decision and label availability times; purge and embargo kept as two separate meanings; accounts carried through in WF, new capital for each CPCV path, new capital for each segment; folds and paths are diagnostics (outside the attempt counts). The ML factor has only the fit/predict contract, the training window enforced by the engine, prediction and fit tables without pickle and a comparison of rerun retraining hashes, single thread by default. The triple-barrier label is in v1 by reusing the exit decision function |
| 0040 | Parallelism in v1 by processes, Python 3.14 free threading a slot swapped in after measurement [R5-04, user decision 2026-10-28] | v1 runs sweeps, CPCV paths and markets side by side with Python 3.13 + worker processes (`[compute] workers`). The free-threaded build of Python 3.14 (3.14t, no GIL) is officially supported, and Numba supports 3.14t from 0.65, but C-extension compatibility has not yet spread through the whole ecosystem. So the parallel executor is a seam where two implementations, `ProcessExecutor` / `ThreadExecutor`, swap in behind the same interface (with a result bit-identity test), and when every dependency in the locked environment ships 3.14t wheels and the threaded version wins in memory and time in the P5, P6 and P11 measurements, the ADR is amended to change the default. In the threaded version too, kernels keep `parallel=False` and the fixed reduction order (5.3.10). |
| 0033 | The default label is the economic holding return | The adjusted-price ratio is reinvestment at the theoretical ex-dividend price, so it differs from the real holding return (100 → dividend 10 → 100 gives 10% vs 11.1%). Default `fwd_return` = reinvestment at the ex-date close (CRSP style), `fwd_return_adj` for comparison. Three label times purge at segment ends. [RV-C04, RV-D2] |

### 9.4 Step 3: the plan issue and the tickets

1. The first M0 ticket (T0.1) puts this spec in as `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md` and TDS v1.4 as `tds/spec/tds-v1.md` (the content as it is; only the name strings adjusted to the repository name; public editions without private comments, TDS chapter 11, the user's personal information, hosts and data paths, 2.7).
2. User: `claude plugin install mattpocock-skills-extra@plinth` (once) → new session → `/to-spec docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md`. One plan issue ("spec: NexusBT v1 (spec v3.4)") [R6-02] is created. Body = the scope of chapter 1, the milestone table of chapter 10, the open items of chapter 12, and a link to the spec file.
3. `/to-tickets` on that plan issue. The tickets of chapter 10 are already cut, so they become issues as they are: title = ticket title, body = Context / Done means / Acceptance tests / Spec section, labels = milestone (`M0`~`M6`, `M3a`/`M3b`) + module, "Blocked by #N" = the issue number of the dependency ticket. If a ticket has to be split again, keep the same form (the 400-line rule). Before making the issues, run `tests/repo/test_ticket_graph.py` once (it reads the chapter 10 table of this spec and checks that the dependency graph is acyclic and that each ticket's "Blocked by" is in the same or an earlier milestone) (made by T0.12).
4. For each milestone, create a GitHub milestone `M0 contract` … `M6 performance`, `v1.x`, and keep the "end conditions" (chapter 10) and the "commands to show" as a checklist in the milestone tracking issue.
5. Producer-side work is handled separately in the producer repository (not this engine repository).

### 9.5 Step 4: `/implement #N` rules (what the agent keeps)

- After `/clear`, one ticket per session. At the start, read only `CONTEXT.md`, the ticket and the spec sections the ticket points to.
- Tests first (tdd): create the test names of the ticket's "Acceptance tests" as they are, see them fail, then implement. Golden cases (`tests/golden/cases/**`) are the ones the preceding golden ticket already merged, and they are **not changed** (8.2); if a case differs from the spec, stop and write "spec deviation".
- Deep modules: change or use only the public interfaces of 2.4. Do not make public a name that is not in the public API (chapter 3). Do not import the `_` internals of another module (import rule test).
- If something has to differ from the spec, stop implementing, write it in the PR description as "spec deviation", and submit an ADR draft with it. Do not make it green by widening tolerances or skipping tests.
- PR description: evidence for "Done means" (commands and part of the output), what `/code-review` found, what could not be confirmed, the `Assisted-by` line. Update the status of the relevant R in `docs/requirements.md`.
- If it looks like it will go over 400 lines, stack PRs (the lower PR is the base of the upper one) — leave an issue comment that splits the ticket in two.

### 9.6 Steps 5~6: "green + seen with one's own eyes"
- Green = the plinth required checks + the `engine / *` checks made required by then.
- Seen = the user (or `/run`) runs the ticket's "Done means" commands and the output matches the description. At the end of a milestone, run the "commands to show" of chapter 10 and merge the PR that commits the evidence files (comparison JSON, performance JSON).
- Default targets of `/run`: `uv run nexusbt doctor` (without data, it points to the synthetic snapshot), and per milestone `uv run python examples/m<k>_demo.py`.

### 9.7 Step 7: when something breaks (`diagnosing-bugs`)
1. Reproduce: the smallest synthetic snapshot — `make_snapshot(tmp, preset="tiny", events=[...], degrade=[...])` + a few lines of strategy. If it happens only on real data, look at the relevant rows with `nexusbt explain` and in the run folder's `orders.parquet` and `account_events.parquet`, and move **only the shape** into tiny synthetic events (no real values or instrument codes in tests, 2.7). For a data format problem, first `tds check --level full`.
2. Failing test: as a hand-calculated case (`tests/golden/cases/`) or a unit test.
3. Fix: the test passes and the other tests stay as they are. Fixes that widen tolerances or `KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md` are not accepted without an ADR.
4. For a zipline comparison difference, first see whether it is in the "known differences table"; if not, check the time alignment (8.1), the rounded inputs and the sid order. Differences between our OHLC rules and zipline are not bugs (scope of 8.1).

### 9.8 Steps 8~9: stopping, coming back, health
- Before stopping: the remaining work in the description of the PR in progress, the checklist of the milestone tracking issue updated, decisions in ADRs, `/handoff`.
- Sources for the answer to "Where is this project, and what's next?" when coming back: the milestone tracking issue, the "Blocked by" of open tickets, `docs/requirements.md` (implemented/deferred/replaced + PR number per R), `bench/results/`, `tests/parity/evidence/`.
- Health: numpy, numba, pyarrow and polars are tied to each other, so one per PR (check the numpy range numba supports). The pinned zipline is the comparison reference, so it is not upgraded (if it is: an ADR + the comparison evidence again). After a plinth template update, `/plinth:floor-check`.

**When spec changes are reflected**: v3 put blueprint SC-01~SC-27 and the P0·P1 (and cheap P2) of the three-reviewer consensus into the body (appendix E). When a spec gap appears during implementation, it is fixed through the "spec deviation" procedure of 9.5 and this document is revised — v2's "there will be no further spec changes before a milestone starts" was removed because the three-reviewer review refuted it [RV-I07]. The ticket where each SC is first implemented·tested is in appendix B.2, RV items in appendix B.4.

**Ticket rules**: one ticket = one session = one PR, changed lines ≤ 400 (excluding docs·lockfiles; plinth `ci / diff-size`). Size S ≤ 150 lines, M ≤ 400 lines. The title is a Conventional Commits title also used as the PR title. "Context" is the work, "Done means" is what a person looks at and confirms, "Acceptance" is the names of the tests to write (made to fail first with tdd), "Blocked by" is ticket numbers (replaced by issue numbers once made into issues). Tickets changed from v1 carry *(v2: …)*, tickets changed in v3 carry *(v3: …)*, new tickets in v2 carry **[new]**, and new·split tickets in v3 carry **[v3]**. **Golden tickets** (`T<m>.G…`) are hand-written per-session arithmetic (8.2) and are merged before the implementation tickets that use them, in a different session. New·split tickets of v3.1 carry **[v3.1]**, changed ones *(v3.1: …)*. New·split tickets of v3.2 carry **[v3.2]**, changed ones *(v3.2: …)*. New·split tickets of v3.3 carry **[v3.3]**, changed ones *(v3.3: …)*. **Engine ticket count (current = v3.4 final + R5, counted by `tools/spec_lint.py`) [R6-04]: M0 16, M1 40, M2 30, M3a 36, M3b 11, M4 28, M5 24, M6 31 (including T7.G·T7.1·T7.V), v1.x 2 (T7.2·T7.5) = 218.** Test·decision ids by the same count: hand-calculation answers G-01~G-106 (106), property tests 34 (table 8.3), errors E1~E49 (49, 6.6), ADR 0001~0040 (40, 9.3). The other numbers in this paragraph and below are records from their version. **Engine ticket count (v3.3): M0 16, M1 40, M2 28, M3a 36, M3b 10, M4 26, M5 22, M6 27, v1.x section: 5 (T7.G·T7.1 at the end of M6, T7.V a check step after T7.1, T7.2·T7.5 in v1.x) = 210** (24 new from v3.2's 188; T7.3·T7.4, moved from v1.x to v1, are absorbed into new tickets T3.29·T3.33 [R4-27]). The numbers in the v3.2 sentence below are records from v3.2. Engine ticket count (v3.2): M0 15, M1 40, M2 28, M3a 28, M3b 9, M4 26, M5 18, M6 17, v1.x section: 7 (T7.G·T7.1 at the end of M6, T7.V a check step after T7.1, T7.2~T7.5 in v1.x) = 188 (from v3.1's 176, 8 by splitting·4 new, mapping table below) [R3-23]. The numbers in the v3.1 sentence below are records from v3.1. Engine ticket count (v3.1): M0 13, M1 39, M2 27, M3a 28, M3b 9, M4 21, M5 16, M6 17, v1.x section: 6 (T7.G·T7.1 go at the end of M6 and T7.2~T7.5 in v1.x) = **176** (34 new from v3's 142 = 141 + tax T4.5b) [V31, V31-LS]. The numbers in the v3 sentence below are records from v3: M0 13, M1 34, M2 23, M3a 22, M3b 7, M4 12, M5 9, M6 16, v1.x 5 = **141** (grown from v2's 112 by splits·new tickets·golden tickets; v2's T0.11·T0.17 were scattered into golden tickets and are gone, and T6.1 moved to T1.24). The dependency graph is acyclic and every "Blocked by" is in the same or an earlier milestone (checked by script when v3 was written; in the repository, T0.12's `test_ticket_graph`).

### 10.2 Producer-side tickets (left out of the public edition)

This section is a private producer's work plan and was left out of the public edition. It has nothing to do with engine behaviour, and the engine's M0~M6 can be finished with the synthetic package (4.8). What the engine does with columns and datasets the producer has not filled is set by the fallback behaviour of 4.5.
